concave

direct

a

All estimators maximize a concave function in
r
, but differ in how the nuisance parameters
\alpha
affect the penalty choice and evaluation over
\alpha
.

5.2
Rates of Convergence with Covariates

The intuition from the no-covariate case extends to the with-covariate case. We therefore focus on the localized first-order estimator
\hat{r}^{\mathrm{FO}}(\cdot,x)
. The analysis builds upon the no-covariate case but must additionally control approximation errors from the local approximation of
r_{0}(X)
and
\alpha_{0}(X)
.
Theorem
5.5
provides rates of convergence in the with-covariate case, which depends on both
\lambda_{x}
and the estimation errors for the marginal probabilities:

Theorem 5.5

Suppose Assumptions
3
–
3
hold, with probability at least
1-\delta
where
\delta\geq\max\left\{\exp(-C^{\prime}\{\sqrt{\log M}\sqrt{Nh^{d}}+Nh^{d}\}),\frac{\log(M)}{\delta_{N}Nh^{d}}\right\}
and
\frac{\log^{3}(pd/\delta)}{Nh^{d}}=\mathrm{o}(1)
, we have
\lambda_{x}/c\geq C_{1}\underline{\lambda}^{\mathrm{FO}}(x)
for some constant
C_{1}>1
,
\delta_{N}\geq\underline{\delta}_{N,x}
where
\underline{\lambda}^{\mathrm{FO}}(x)
and
\underline{\delta}_{N,x}
are defined as (
29
) and (
30
). Let
r_{*x}(X):=r_{0}(x)+\nabla_{x}r_{0}(x)(X-x)
. Then with probability at least
1-C\delta-C\delta_{\alpha}-\frac{C(\bar{K}_{\max}/\bar{K}_{2})^{2}}{Nh^{d}}-C\epsilon
,

\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{K_{h}(X_{i}-x)\{W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{FO}}(X_{i},x)-r_{*x}(X_{i}))\}^{2}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i}))^{2}}}\leq C\frac{\lambda_{x}\sqrt{s}}{\kappa_{x,\bar{c}}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{1/2}(\hat{\alpha}),

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})\leq C\overline{R}_{x}
, and
C
is a universal constant independent of
s,M,N,h,d
, and
\overline{R}_{x}
is defined as (
31
).

The convergence rate established in Theorem
5.5
depends on the estimation error of
\alpha_{0}(\cdot)
in the adversarial set through the bound
\overline{R}_{x}
, making accurate estimation of
\alpha_{0}(\cdot)
essential. Given the smoothness condition in Assumption
3
(3), local linear regression provides a natural approach. Specifically, for each
j\in[M]
, we define

\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{j}(x),\tilde{\beta}_{j}(x)\right)\in\arg\min_{\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{R},\tilde{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times d}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}K_{h}(X_{i}-x)\left\{Y_{ij}-\tilde{a}-\tilde{b}(X_{i}-x)\right\}^{2}.

(10)

Since
\alpha_{0}(x)\in[\eta,1-\eta]
by Assumption
3
, we truncate
\tilde{\alpha}_{j}(x)
and set

\hat{\alpha}_{j}(x):=\min\{\max\{\eta,\tilde{\alpha}_{j}(x)\},1-\eta\},\ \ \forall j\in[M].

(11)

To establish the theoretical properties of this local linear estimator, we impose Assumption
5.2
:

{assumption}

(i)
\int uu^{T}K(u)du=\mu_{2}\mathbf{I}_{d}
, where
\mu_{2}\neq 0
is a scalar and
\mathbf{I}_{d}
is the
d\times d
identity matrix.
(ii)
K(\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is supported on
\{u:\|u\|\leq 1\}
. For any
\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
,
K(-z)=K(z)
and
\underline{\kappa}\leq K(z)\leq\mathcal{K}
, where
\underline{\kappa}
,
\mathcal{K}
are positive absolute constants.

Assumption
5.2
is standard in the local linear regression literature
(
Ruppert and Wand 1994
,
Fan 1993
,
Lu 1996
,
Fan and Yao 2003
, e.g.)
and is satisfied by many commonly used kernels, including the uniform (box) kernel
K(u)=\mathbf{1}\{\|u\|\leq 1\}
and the quadratic-positive kernel
K(u)=\mu_{0}(1+\mu_{1}\|u\|^{2})\mathbf{1}\{\|u\|\leq 1\}
. Since
K(\cdot)
is supported on the unit Euclidean ball by Assumption
5.2
(ii), for any
X_{i}
such that
K_{h}(X_{i}-x)\neq 0
,
\mathrm{O}\left(\|X_{i}-x\|^{2}\right)=\mathrm{O}(h^{2})
. Recall that smoothness of the marginal probability implies
\alpha_{0}(X_{i})=\alpha_{0}(x)+\nabla\alpha_{0}(x)(X_{i}-x)+\Delta_{x,X_{i}}
and
\|\Delta_{x,X_{i}}\|_{\infty}=\mathrm{O}\left(\|X_{i}-x\|^{2}\right)
. Hence,
\alpha_{*x}(X_{i})=\alpha_{0}(x)+\nabla_{x}\alpha_{0}(x)(X_{i}-x)
effectively approximates
\alpha_{0}(X_{i})
when
X_{i}
is close enough to
x
, where
\tilde{\alpha}_{j}(x)
approximates
\alpha_{0j}(x)
and
\tilde{\beta}_{j}(x)
approximates its gradient
\nabla\alpha_{0j}(x)
. This local linear approximation affects the analysis but its impact is well controlled.

We next derive the convergence rate of the first-order estimator in the with-covariate case using the adversarial set
\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
defined in (
14
); details of its construction are provided in Section
5.3
.

Corollary 5.6

Suppose the conditions of Theorem
5.5
and Assumption
5.2
hold,
\lambda_{x}/c\leq C_{2}\underline{\lambda}^{\mathrm{FO}}(x)
for some constant
C_{2}>C_{1}>1
,
\kappa_{x,\bar{c}}
is bounded away from zero and
\bar{K}_{2}+K_{\alpha,1}+K_{\alpha,2}/\xi^{2}\leq C^{\prime}
for some constant
C^{\prime}
. Let the adversarial set
\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
be constructed as (
14
), define
\Delta_{x,i}^{\mathrm{FO}}:=\frac{W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{FO}}(X_{i},x)-r_{*x}(X_{i}))}{1+W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i})}
. Then with probability
1-C\delta-C\delta_{\alpha}-C\epsilon-C(\bar{K}_{\max}/\bar{K}_{2})^{2}/(Nh^{d})
where
0<\delta\leq\delta_{\alpha}
, we have

\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}K_{h}(X_{i}-x)\left(\Delta_{x,i}^{\mathrm{FO}}\right)^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{sM+s\log(1/\delta)}{Nh^{d}}}+C(s+\sqrt{M})dh^{2},

(12)

where
C
is a universal constant independent of
s,N,M,h,d
. The optimal bandwidth choice is
h^{*}=\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\delta_{N}\xi}{sd}},\max\left\{\left(\frac{M^{3}}{N\delta_{N}^{2}}\right)^{1/d},4^{-\frac{2}{d+4}}\left(\frac{sM+s\log(1/\delta)}{N(s+\sqrt{M})^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{d+4}}\right\}\right\}
, under which with probability
1-C\delta-C\delta_{\alpha}-C\epsilon-C(\bar{K}_{\max}/\bar{K}_{2})^{2}/[N(h^{*})^{d}]
where
0<\delta\leq\delta_{\alpha}
, we have
\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}K_{h}(X_{i}-x)\left(\Delta_{x,i}^{\mathrm{FO}}\right)^{2}}\leq\bar{C}\min\left\{B_{1},B_{2}\right\}
, where
B_{1}:=\delta_{N}\sqrt{\frac{s(M+\log(1/\delta))}{M^{3}}}+(\sqrt{M}+s)d\left(\frac{M^{3}}{N\delta_{N}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{d}}
,

B_{2}:=\sqrt{\frac{s(M+\log(1/\delta))}{N}}\left(\frac{sd}{\delta_{N}\xi}\right)^{d/4}\!\!+\frac{(\sqrt{M}+s)\delta_{N}\xi}{s}
, and
\bar{C}
is a constant independent of
s,N,M,h,d
.

The first term on the right-hand side of (
12
) is
\mathrm{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{s\log(p/\delta)}{Nh^{d}}}\right)
, which is the typical localized Lasso rate with known
\alpha_{0}(\cdot)
. The second term, which is
\mathrm{O}((s+\sqrt{M})dh^{2})
, captures the approximation error induced by localization. Under the smoothness conditions, the local-linear approximations of
r_{0}(\cdot)
and
\alpha_{0}(\cdot)
yield second-order Taylor remainders of order
dh^{2}
. These errors accumulate over the active components of
r_{0}(\cdot)
yielding
sdh^{2}
, and across the
M
marginal-probability functions in
\alpha_{0}(\cdot)
yielding
\sqrt{M}dh^{2}
.

Ignoring the bandwidth constraints, the first-order condition for minimizing the right-hand side of (
12
) yields
h_{0}=4^{-\frac{2}{d+4}}\left(\frac{sM+s\log(1/\delta)}{N(s+\sqrt{M})^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{d+4}}
. Recall that Assumption
3
requires
h\in[h_{L},h_{U}]
, where
h_{L}=\left(M^{3}/(N\delta_{N}^{2})\right)^{1/d}
and
h_{U}=\sqrt{\delta_{N}\xi/(sd)}
. Hence the optimal bandwidth
h^{*}=\min\{h_{U},\max\{h_{L},h_{0}\}\}
. The quantities
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
are the values of the rate bound evaluated at the two endpoints of this feasible interval:
B_{1}
corresponds to
h=h_{L}
, whereas
B_{2}
corresponds to
h=h_{U}
. Because
h^{*}
minimizes the bound over
[h_{L},h_{U}]
, its rate is bounded by
\bar{C}\min\{B_{1},B_{2}\}
. If neither bandwidth constraint binds, then
h^{*}=h_{0}
and for fixed
s
,
M
,
d
,
\delta
, the resulting prediction error has order
N^{-2/(d+4)}
, which is the classical pointwise convergence rate for estimating a twice-smooth function using local linear regression.

5.3
Construction of the Adversarial Sets

This section details the construction of the adversarial sets for the no-covariate and with-covariate cases. The key idea is to construct
\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
and
\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
as hyper-rectangles, so that the minimum of the inner optimization problem with respect to
\alpha
in the first order estimators is attained at one of their finitely many extreme points, thereby enabling efficient computation. Recall that for the no-covariate case, we consider (
4
) as the initial estimator for the marginal probabilities. We construct the adversarial set in the no-covariate case as

\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha}):=\prod_{j=1}^{M}[\hat{\alpha}_{j}-cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}\hat{s}_{j},\hat{\alpha}_{j}+cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}\hat{s}_{j}],

(13)

where
\hat{s}_{j}=\sqrt{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}
and
cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}:=(1-\delta_{\alpha})\mbox{-quantile of}\max_{j\in[M]}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{i}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}{\sqrt{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}}\right|
conditioning on
\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
for
\delta_{\alpha}\in(0,1)
, and
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
random variables.

For the with-covariate case, recall that we use local linear regression estimators (
10
) and (
11
) for the marginal probabilities. We construct
\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
as a set of linear functions to contain the function
\alpha_{*x}(X_{i})=\alpha_{0}(x)+\nabla_{x}\alpha_{0}(x)(X_{i}-x)
with high probability. Specifically,

\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha}):=\prod_{j=1}^{M}\left\{\!\alpha_{j}+\beta_{j}(X-x):\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{l}\alpha_{j}\in[\hat{\alpha}_{j}(x)-cv_{x}\hat{s}_{j,0}(x),\hat{\alpha}_{j}(x)+cv_{x}\hat{s}_{j,0}(x)],\\
\beta_{jk}\in[\tilde{\beta}_{jk}(x)-cv_{x}\hat{s}_{j,k}(x),\tilde{\beta}_{jk}(x)+cv_{x}\hat{s}_{j,k}(x)],k\in[d]\end{array}\!\!\right\},

(14)

and conditional on
\{Y_{i},X_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
,
cv_{x}:=(1-\delta_{\alpha})\mbox{-quantile of }{\displaystyle\max_{j\in[M],\ell\in\{0\}\cup[d]}}\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{i}\frac{\hat{\epsilon}_{ij}\hat{\zeta}_{i\ell}}{\hat{s}_{j,\ell}(x)}\right|
, where
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
random variables,
\hat{\epsilon}_{ij}:=Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j}(x)-\tilde{\beta}_{j}(x)(X_{i}-x)
,
\hat{\zeta}_{i}:=(Z^{\prime}W_{x}Z)^{-1}Z_{i}K_{h}(X_{i}-x)
with
Z_{i}=(1,(X_{i}-x)^{\prime})^{\prime}
,
Z=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{N})^{\prime}
and
W_{x}
is a diagonal
N\times N
matrix with its
i
-th diagonal entry equal to
W_{x,ii}:=K_{h}(X_{i}-x)
,
\hat{s}_{j,\ell}(x):=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\epsilon}_{ij}^{2}\hat{\zeta}_{i\ell}^{2}}
, and
\hat{\zeta}_{i\ell}
is the
\ell
-th component of vector
\hat{\zeta}_{i}
,
\forall\ell\in\{0\}\cup[d]
.

6
Application: Multiple Binary Treatments

In this section, we apply our theoretical results to the estimation of average treatment effects in the presence of
M
binary treatments. At a high level, we show that exploiting sparsity or independence among treatments enables our estimator to estimate the generalized propensity scores of treatment combinations more efficiently.

We consider a standard potential outcome framework
(
Imbens and Rubin 2015
)
. For a treatment vector
t\in\{0,1\}^{M}
, the
j
th treatment is treated if and only if
t_{j}=1
. We observe the triplets
Z_{i}=(O_{i},T_{i},X_{i})
across
i\in[N]
, where
O_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
denotes the outcome of interest,
T_{i}\in\{0,1\}^{M}
denotes the treatment assignments, and
X_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the covariate. We are interested in estimating the
average treatment effect
(ATE) between any two treatment levels
t,t^{\prime}
of interest, namely
\tau(t,t^{\prime})=\mathbb{E}[O(t)-O(t^{\prime})]
, where
O(t)
denotes the potential outcome of receiving the treatment
t\in\{0,1\}^{M}
. We consider the cross-fitted
augmented inverse probability weighted
(AIPW) estimator
(
Robins et al. 1994
,
Robins and Rotnitzky 1995
,
Hahn 1998
,
Scharfstein et al. 1999
,
Kennedy 2020
,
Newey and Robins 2018
, e.g.)
.
Specifically, we split the data into
K
disjoint folds of size
N/K
and we use
\mathcal{I}_{k}
to denote the
k
-th fold, for
k\in[K]
. It is not required that all the folds have the same size, but we proceed with this merely for simplicity, without loss of generality. For any
t\in\{0,1\}^{M}
, we let
\hat{\mu}_{(t)}^{-k}(X)
and
\hat{e}_{t}^{-k}(X)
denote the estimators for
\mu_{(t)}(X)=\mathbb{E}[O|T=t,X]
and
e_{t}(X)=\mathbb{P}(T=t|X)
without using observations from fold
k
. Then we estimate the ATE by

\hat{\tau}(t,t^{\prime})=\!\frac{1}{K}\!\!\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{I}_{k}|}\!\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\!\left(\hat{\mu}_{(t)}^{-k}(X_{i})\!-\!\hat{\mu}_{(t^{\prime})}^{-k}(X_{i})\!+\!\frac{\mathbf{1}\{T_{i}=t\}}{\hat{e}_{t}^{-k}(X_{i})}\!\!\left(O_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{(t)}^{-k}(X_{i})\right)-\frac{1\{T_{i}=t^{\prime}\}}{\hat{e}_{t^{\prime}}^{-k}(X_{i})}\!\!\left(O_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{(t^{\prime})}^{-k}(X_{i})\right)\right)
.
The generalized propensity scores are estimated based on (
11
) and the first-order estimator
\hat{r}^{\mathrm{FO}}(\cdot,x)
at the given covariate of interest
x
:
\hat{e}_{t}(x)=\Big(1+W(\hat{\alpha}(x),t)^{\prime}\hat{r}^{\mathrm{FO}}(x,x)\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{M}\hat{\alpha}_{j}(x)^{t_{j}}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j}(x))^{1-t_{j}}
. We assume that the sample contains observations with treatment
t
as well as observations receiving treatment
t^{\prime}
. We impose the following regularity conditions:

{assumption}

Suppose (i)
\{O_{i}(a)\}_{a\in\{t,t^{\prime}\}}\perp\!\!\!\perp T_{i}\mid X_{i}
; (ii) there is a constant
c\in(0,1)
such that
c\prod_{j=1}^{M}\alpha_{0j}(x)^{a_{j}}(1-\alpha_{0j}(x))^{1-a_{j}}<e_{a}(x)<1-c,\forall x\in\mathcal{X}
, for any
a\in\{t,t^{\prime}\}
. (iii) For any
a\in\{t,t^{\prime}\}
,
\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}|\hat{\mu}_{(a)}(x)|\leq\bar{\mu},\max_{i\in[N]}|O_{i}(a)|\leq\bar{\mu}
for some absolute constant
\bar{\mu}>0
,
\mathbb{E}[\{\hat{\mu}_{(a)}(X_{i})-\mu_{(a)}(X_{i})\}^{2}]\leq\epsilon_{N}^{2}N^{-1+2\gamma}
,
\max_{a\in\{t,t^{\prime}\}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\{\hat{\mu}_{(a)}(X_{i})-\mu_{(a)}(X_{i})\}^{2}}\leq\epsilon_{N}N^{-1/2+\gamma}
with probability
1-\epsilon_{N}
for some
0<\gamma\leq 1/4
, where
\epsilon_{N}\to 0
.

Assumption
6
(i) imposes a standard unconfoundedness condition. Assumption
6
(ii) imposes a dimension-adaptive overlap requirement: each target configuration must occur with probability at least a constant fraction of the probability it would have under conditionally independent assignment with the same marginal probabilities. Because this lower bound in (ii) decreases exponentially with
M
, the condition allows
e_{a}(x)
to decrease with
M
while ruling out configurations that are disproportionately rare relative to their marginals. This distinction becomes particularly important when the analysis is extended to the setting where many contrasts are considered, because the conventional fixed lower bound on
e_{a}(x)
cannot hold uniformly over many binary configurations as
M
grows. When the propensity score
e_{a}(x)
is positive but the lower bound in Assumption
6
(ii) is violated, large inverse-probability
weights can make the ATE estimate sensitive to a few observations.
Truncating these weights limits their influence but may introduce
bias. Alternatively, restricting or reweighting the target population
to emphasize covariate regions with greater treatment overlap changes
the target estimand
(
Crump et al. 2009
,
Li and Li 2019
, e.g.,)
.
If
e_{a}(x)=0
on a covariate region of positive probability so treatment

a
is never observed in that region, the full-population ATE involving
this treatment is therefore generally not identified without additional
assumptions. Possible remedies include restricting the target population or treatment contrast, model-based extrapolation, or partial identification.
Assumption
6
(iii) requires the outcome regression estimators to satisfy boundedness
and convergence-rate conditions.

Theorem 6.1

Suppose Assumptions
3
–
6
hold. Suppose
\kappa_{x,\bar{c}}
is bounded away from zero,
\bar{K}_{2}+K_{\alpha,1}+K_{\alpha,2}/\xi^{2}\leq C^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
is constructed as before. Additionally, suppose for some
\psi_{N}
and
0<\gamma\leq\frac{1}{4}
,
\psi_{N}N^{\gamma}<1
,
\psi_{N}N^{\gamma}\to 0
, and for some constant
C
,

\sqrt{s}\left(\sqrt{\frac{sM}{Nh^{d}}}\!+(s+\sqrt{M})dh^{2}\right)\leq\frac{C\psi_{N}}{\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X},a\in\{t,t^{\prime}\}}\!\prod_{j=1}^{M}\!\alpha_{0j}(x)^{a_{j}}(1-\alpha_{0j}(x))^{1-a_{j}}},

(15)

Mdh^{2}+\frac{M}{\sqrt{Nh^{d}}}\leq C\psi_{N},

(16)

where
M=\mathrm{o}(\min\{\log(1/\psi_{N}),\log(1/\epsilon_{N})\})
, then
\sqrt{N}\hat{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\hat{\tau}(t,t^{\prime})-\tau(t,t^{\prime}))\to\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, where
\hat{\Sigma}
is the semiparametric efficiency variance bound.
\blacksquare

Theorem
6.1
shows that treatment-induced sparsity in
r_{0}(x)
allows the generalized propensity estimator to satisfy the nuisance-rate conditions required for semiparametrically efficient AIPW inference when
s\ll 2^{M}
under the stated growing-dimensional regime. The semiparametric efficiency calculation itself follows standard arguments for AIPW estimators
(
Wager 2020
,
Imbens and Rubin 2015
, e.g.,)
, and our contribution lies in the generalized propensity estimator and its growing-dimensional rate guarantees. Although Theorem
6.1
is stated for a prespecified pair
(t,t^{\prime})
, it applies to any fixed pair satisfying the stated conditions. This matters when inference is required for an arbitrary contrast among a large collection of candidate treatment combinations. We discussed above that Assumption
6
(ii) accommodates this setting. Remark
6.2
shows the compatibility of the conditions in Theorem
6.1
and provide two examples of
(N,M,d,s)
satisfying them.

Remark 6.2

We illustrate the compatibility of
Assumption
3
and the conditions of
Theorem
6.1
.
Fix the covariate dimension
d\geq 1
and
\xi>0
. Suppose
0<\gamma\leq\frac{1}{4},\gamma<\frac{2}{d+4}
, and choose a constant
\eta
satisfying

\gamma<\eta<2/(d+4)
. Set
M=\left\lceil(\log N)^{3/4}\right\rceil,\psi_{N}=N^{-\eta},\epsilon_{N}=N^{-\gamma/2}
, choose
h=\left(\frac{\log(NMd)}{Nd^{2}}\right)^{1/(d+4)},\delta_{N}=\frac{M^{3/2}}{\sqrt{Nh^{d}}}
.
Two admissible values of sparsity parameter
s
are
s=\lceil\log M\rceil
and
s=\lceil\sqrt{M}\rceil
. For the rest of this remark, we show that the given choices of
(s,N,M,d,h)
satisfy all the imposed conditions.

First,

\psi_{N}N^{\gamma}=N^{\gamma-\eta}\to 0
and
M=\mathrm{o}\!\left(\min\{\log(1/\psi_{N}),\log(1/\epsilon_{N})\}\right)
. Write

v_{N}:=(Nh^{d})^{-1/2}
and
b_{N}:=dh^{2}
.
The bandwidth choice gives
b_{N}=\sqrt{\log(NMd)}\,v_{N},M(v_{N}+b_{N})=\mathrm{o}(\psi_{N})
. This verifies (
16
).
For either value of
s
,
\sqrt{s}\left(\sqrt{\frac{sM}{Nh^{d}}}+(s+\sqrt{M})dh^{2}\right)=s\sqrt{M}\,v_{N}+\sqrt{s}(s+\sqrt{M})b_{N}=\mathrm{O}\!\left(M(v_{N}+b_{N})\right)=\mathrm{o}(\psi_{N})
. Since the product of marginal probabilities in the denominator
of (
15
) is at most one,
that condition also holds. Finally, the chosen
\delta_{N}
equals the third upper bound
in Assumption
3
, and
\frac{sdh^{2}}{\xi\delta_{N}}=\frac{s\sqrt{\log(NMd)}}{\xi M^{3/2}}\rightarrow 0
for both sparsity regimes. Consequently,
M^{3}=Nh^{d}\delta_{N}^{2}\leq N\delta_{N}^{2+d/2}\left(\frac{\xi}{sd}\right)^{d/2}
. The remaining requirements follow from
Nh^{d+4}=\frac{\log(NMd)}{d^{2}}
,
\delta_{N}\to 0
,
\frac{\delta_{N}d^{3}\xi}{s}\to 0,d^{2}h\to 0,Nh^{d}\to\infty
. The restriction
\gamma<2/(d+4)
is equivalent to

d<2/\gamma-4
. In particular,
\gamma=1/4
requires

d\leq 3
, whereas smaller values of
\gamma
permit larger
fixed covariate dimensions.

\blacksquare

Our result is related to the doubly robust kernel estimators. The condition in standard semiparametric settings requires
\mathrm{o}_{\mathrm{P}}(N^{-1/4})
consistency
(
Chernozhukov et al. 2017a
, e.g.)
. For non-parametric methods,
Kennedy et al. (2017)
estimates continuous treatment effect and derives
\sqrt{N}
-local rate of convergence with a kernel smoothing approach, by requiring that
h=\mathrm{O}(N^{-1/5})
, and the outcome estimation and propensity score estimation are both required to be
\mathrm{o}_{\mathrm{P}}(N^{-1/5})
.
Rothe and Firpo (2019)
proposes a semiparametric two-step doubly robust estimators by requiring
\mathrm{o}_{\mathrm{P}}(N^{-1/6})
consistency for both outcome and propensity score estimation, as long as their product is
\mathrm{o}_{\mathrm{P}}(N^{-1/2})
.
Our estimator allows a slower rate of convergence for estimating the generalized propensity scores by imposing additional sparsity assumptions with a prudent choice of
N,M,d,h
.

In the Electronic Companion, Section
EC.3.3
reports numerical results for
M=4
, while Section
EC.3.2
presents results for a causal-inference setting that estimates the ATEs of all 15 noncontrol treatment configurations relative to the control
(0,0,0,0)
. Across values of
N
and
s
spanning sparse to dense settings, AIPW estimators using generalized propensity scores from our methods outperform those based on the MNL and Nadaraya–Watson estimators. Further details are provided there.

7
Numerical Simulations

We construct a synthetic product bundle purchase scenario with
M=15
, so
p=2^{15}-15-1=32{,}752
. Each observation
i
represents a customer’s shopping decision:
Y_{ij}=1
indicates that category
j
is purchased, and
Y_{i}\in\{0,1\}^{15}
records the resulting bundle from fifteen categories: milk, bread, eggs, butter, cereal, coffee, tea, cookies, pasta, pasta sauce, cheese, yogurt, chips, salsa, and beer. Each customer has
d=3
covariates
x_{i}=(x_{i1},x_{i2},x_{i3})\in[0,1]^{3}
, representing percentiles of household size, income, and the age of the shopper. We draw a latent vector
\xi_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,C)
, where
C
has unit diagonal entries
C_{11}=C_{22}=C_{33}=1
, and off-diagonal entries
C_{12}=C_{21}=0.25
,
C_{23}=C_{32}=0.30
,
C_{13}=C_{31}=-0.20
, corresponding to household size–income, income–age, and household size–age dependence. We set
x_{ik}=\Phi(\xi_{ik})
, where
\Phi
is the standard normal CDF. We specify each product category’s conditional purchase probability using the logistic specification

\alpha_{0j}(x)=1/\{1+\exp[-(\theta_{j0}+\theta_{j}^{\top}x)]\}
.
Let
\bar{x}=(0.5,0.5,0.5)
denote the reference profile at which each percentile-transformed covariate equals its marginal median. For each category, we choose the slope coefficients
\theta_{j}
and calibrate the intercept
\theta_{j0}
so that the purchase probabilities remain within
[0.2,0.8]
over the covariate cube. The generalized correlation coefficient vector
r_{0}(x)
is supported on
s=6
bundles:
pasta–sauce
r_{0,\{9,10\}}(x)=0.210+0.016(x_{2}-0.5)
;
chips–salsa
r_{0,\{13,14\}}(x)=0.178-0.0128(x_{3}-0.5)
;
milk–cereal
r_{0,\{1,5\}}(x)=0.136+0.016(x_{1}-0.5)
;
chips–beer
r_{0,\{13,15\}}(x)=0.105-0.0128(x_{3}-0.5)
;
coffee–tea substitutes
r_{0,\{6,7\}}(x)=-0.063-0.0064(x_{3}-0.5)
;
and the third-order milk–bread–eggs breakfast basket
r_{0,\{1,2,3\}}(x)=0.059+0.0096(x_{1}-0.5)
.
All remaining coordinates of
r_{0}(x)
are zero. We verify numerically that
1+\sum_{l}r_{0l}(x)W_{l}(\alpha_{0}(x),y)>0

for all
y\in\{0,1\}^{15}
and all
x
on a
7^{3}
grid of the covariate cube.

The no-covariate case aims at the median customer,

\alpha_{0}=\alpha_{0}(\bar{x})
and
r_{0}=r_{0}(\bar{x})
, whose six nonzero entries are
(0.210,\,0.178,\,0.136,\,0.105,\,-0.063,\,0.059)
following the order listed above. We draw
N=2{,}500
bundles per replication and estimate
r_{0}
, where we use the
sample average approximation
(SAA) estimator as a baseline, whose
l
-th component is equal to
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j\in\ell(l)}z_{j}(Y_{i},\hat{\alpha}_{j}),\forall l\in[p]
. Per replication of the with-covariate case, we draw
N=8{,}000
customers and estimate
r_{0}(x)
at two profiles:

x_{\mathrm{L}}=(0.70,0.50,0.40)
, representing a larger household with median income and a younger shopper, and

x_{\mathrm{S}}=(0.40,0.65,0.60)
, representing a smaller household with higher income and an older shopper. The local-linear regression uses a product uniform kernel and bandwidth
h=0.32
. The local neighborhoods contain on average
2{,}199
and
2{,}242
observations with nonzero kernel weights at
x_{\mathrm{L}}
and
x_{\mathrm{S}}
. The with-covariate case uses the
Nadaraya-Watson
(NW) estimator as baseline, whose
l
-th component is equal to
\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N}K_{h}(X_{i}-x)\prod_{j\in\ell(l)}z_{j}(Y_{i},\hat{\alpha}(X_{i}))}{\sum_{i=1}^{N}K_{h}(X_{i}-x)}
,
\forall l\in[p]
.

We compare the plug-in, first-order and oracle estimators. We consider two penalty-weighting schemes, corresponding to different choices of
\{w_{k}\}_{k\in[2^{M}-M-1]}
in (
3
) and (
6
) in the no-covariate case: (I)
w_{k}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W_{k}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{2}}
, corresponding to the initialization
\hat{r}^{(0)}=0
; (II)
w_{k}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W_{k}^{2}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})}{(1+W_{i}^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}}
, corresponding to the oracle initialization
\hat{r}^{(0)}=r_{0}
. This initialization is infeasible because
r_{0}
is unknown, and we include it as a benchmark. For the with-covariate case: (I)
w_{k}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{K_{h}(X_{i}-x)}{N}W_{k}(\hat{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{2}}
,
w_{k,1}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{K_{h}(X_{i}-x)}{N}W_{k}(\hat{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{2}(X_{i}-x)^{2}}
, corresponding to
\hat{r}^{(0)}(X_{i},x)=0
; (II)
w_{k}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{K_{h}(X_{i}-x)W_{k}(\hat{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{2}}{N[1+W(\hat{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i})]^{2}}}
,
w_{k,1}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{K_{h}(X_{i}-x)W_{k}(\hat{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{2}(X_{i}-x)^{2}}{N[1+W(\hat{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i})]^{2}}}
, corresponding to
\hat{r}^{(0)}(X_{i},x)=r_{0}(X_{i})
. For any
y\in\{0,1\}^{M}
, let
p_{y}
be the true probability of
y
, and
\hat{p}_{y}=(1+W(\hat{\alpha},y)^{\prime}\hat{r})\prod_{j=1}^{M}\hat{\alpha}_{j}^{y_{j}}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})^{1-y_{j}}
. We compute three metrics,
root mean squared error
(RMSE)
\mathbb{E}[\left\lVert\hat{r}-r_{0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}]^{1/2}
,
\mathbb{E}[\max_{y\in\{0,1\}^{M}}|\hat{p}_{y}-p_{y}|]
and
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}^{M}}p_{y}|\hat{p}_{y}-p_{y}|]
. When
\hat{p}_{y}\notin[0,1]
, we truncate it to
[0,1]
. For the no-covariate case,
\lambda_{cv}
denotes the
\lambda
chosen via cross-validation,
\lambda_{\mathrm{theory}}
denotes the theoretically derived penalty parameter, chosen at the scale prescribed by Theorem
5.3
for the first-order estimators and Proposition
5.2
for the plug-in estimators. Specifically,
\lambda_{\mathrm{theory}}^{\mathrm{PI}}=\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/(2p))}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{M}{\sqrt{N}}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{theory}}^{\mathrm{FO}}=\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/(2p))}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{M}{N}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{theory}}^{\mathrm{PI}_{\mathrm{oracle}}}=\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/(2p))}{\sqrt{N}}
. Similarly, for the with-covariate case,
\lambda_{\mathrm{theory}}^{\mathrm{PI}}=\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2p(d+1))}{\sqrt{Nh^{d}}}+\frac{M}{\sqrt{Nh^{d}}}+dMh^{2}
,

\lambda_{\mathrm{theory}}^{\mathrm{FO}}=\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2p(d+1))}{\sqrt{Nh^{d}}}+\frac{M}{Nh^{d}}+Md^{2}h^{4}
,
\lambda_{\mathrm{theory}}^{\mathrm{PI}_{\mathrm{oracle}}}=\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2p(d+1))}{\sqrt{Nh^{d}}}
, where “PI”, “FO” and “oracle” refer to plug-in, first-order and oracle (plug-in) estimators.

and

identify weighting schemes (I) and (II). The first-order estimators yield lower RMSEs and more accurate probability estimates than the plug-in estimator. The oracle plug-in estimator performs best but is generally infeasible because
\alpha_{0}
is unknown. By contrast, the SAA and NW baselines perform substantially worse than the plug-in, first-order, and oracle plug-in estimators.

Computation of the first-order estimator for
M=15
.
In the no-covariate case,
\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
contains
2^{15}=32,768
extreme points and exact enumeration is feasible. In the with-covariate case, the adversarial set is a hyper-rectangle with
2^{M(1+d)}=2^{60}
extreme points, making exhaustive enumeration infeasible. We therefore employ a two-step inexact cutting-set procedure following the heuristics of
Mutapcic and Boyd (2009)
: repeatedly solve a restricted robust problem, search for a more adverse realization, and add it to the restricted problem.

Step 1 (initialize)
: We initialize an active set with two opposite corners of the hyper-rectangle: the all-upper corner, where every
\alpha_{j}
and
\beta_{jk}
is set to the upper endpoint of its confidence interval, and the all-lower corner, where every coefficient is set to its lower endpoint. We then add
100
randomly sampled extreme points by independently selecting the lower or upper endpoint of each coefficient with equal probability.
Step 2 (expand extreme-point set)
: We iteratively expand the collection of extreme points. First, we solve the estimation problem with the inner minimization restricted to the current collection, obtaining
\hat{r}
. Holding
\hat{r}
fixed, we then search for a more adverse extreme point. Within each iteration, we cycle through the coefficients
\alpha_{j}
and
\beta_{jk}
. For each coefficient, we hold all others fixed, compare the inner objective at its lower and upper endpoints, and retain the endpoint yielding the smaller value. We repeat until no single endpoint change decreases the objective and retain the best terminal point. If this point lowers the inner objective at
\hat{r}
by more than the prescribed tolerance relative to the current collection, we add it and re-solve the estimation problem; otherwise, the procedure terminates. Each coordinate update requires one pass over the observations, so a complete iteration over all
M(1+d)
coefficients costs
\mathrm{O}(NM(1+d))
, which is negligible relative to the outer estimation problem.

8
Discussion

We develop a method for estimating binary configuration probabilities. Existing approaches often ignore independence structures in the joint distribution and can therefore be statistically inefficient in high dimensions. Our approach is adaptive to the independence structure by leveraging sparsity in the generalized correlation coefficients
(
Bahadur 1959
)
, yielding improved finite-sample performance both theoretically and numerically. We close the paper by a discussion on possible extensions. First, for the covariate-case, we establish a pointwise rate of convergence result for
r_{0}(x)
. So a natural extension is to establish uniform convergence results over all
x\in\mathcal{X}
. Second, it would also be interesting to incorporate prior information about independence directly into the generalized correlation coefficients and to develop localized sparsity constraints, such as sparsity levels
s_{x}
that vary across covariate strata. We expect our method to remain valid provided that the covariate dimension is low. It is also interesting to incorporate high-dimensional covariates, which would require new modeling strategies and estimators. Further, our approach offers a fundamentally different way to model independence from the graphical conditional independence.
Yuan and Qu (2021)
shows in simulations that their community detection method based on a truncated Bahadur representation outperforms approaches that assume conditional independence. This suggests that our method may also be useful in similar settings, such as community detection, to capture dependence among edges. Lastly, recall from Remark
4.1
that since
(\hat{\alpha},r_{0})
may not lie in
\mathbb{K}
, we do not impose this constraint in the optimization. For the plug-in estimator, enforcing
(\hat{\alpha},r)\in\mathbb{K}
requires adding
2^{M}
linear constraints. For the first-order estimator, this requirement would be even more restrictive, since it would require
(\alpha,r)\in\mathbb{K}
for every
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
, which can be computationally prohibitive. Moreover, this introduces a tradeoff: the feasible set may become overly restrictive, and since it is possible that
(\hat{\alpha},r_{0})\notin\mathbb{K}
, imposing such constraints can lead to large estimation bias. The discussion in Remark
4.1
indicates that our result is sufficient for a broad range of practical applications. For the causal inference application,
\min_{i\in[N]}1+W(\hat{\alpha}(X_{i}),t)^{\prime}\hat{r}^{\mathrm{FO}}(X_{i},X_{i})>0
with high probability, so the AIPW estimator is generally not impacted either. In the rare cases where positivity of the probability estimates fails, a simple truncation step can be applied. There could exist alternative projection-based approaches that could enforce this constraint, but analyzing such methods would require a different type of analysis. Since the current approach is already sufficiently general for many practically relevant settings, so we leave such extensions to future work.

Appendix

We now state Assumption
Appendix
which is implied by Assumption
3
, with the choices
K_{\alpha,1}=2L_{N}
,
\bar{K}_{w}=L_{N}
,
K_{\alpha,2}=2L_{N}^{2}
,
\bar{K}_{x,2}=C_{x}
below:

{assumption}

With probability
1-\delta_{\alpha}
, conditions (1)–(6)
below hold simultaneously for every
i\in[N]
and for all vectors

u,v
of compatible dimensions:

(1)
\alpha_{0}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
,   (2)
{\displaystyle\max_{j\in[p],\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}}|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})|+|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}|\leq M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}\|v\|
,

(3)
|u^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}|\leq\bar{K}_{w}\|u\|_{1}
,
|u^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}v|\leq\bar{K}_{w}\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{1}\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{1}
,

(4)
\displaystyle\max_{j\in[p],\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}|u^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}v|+|u^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})v|\leq K_{\alpha,2}\|u\|\|v\|
,

(5)
{\displaystyle\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}}\|\tilde{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|\frac{M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}}{\xi}<\frac{1}{4}
,
{\displaystyle\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}}\|\tilde{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}\frac{K_{\alpha,2}}{\xi}\leq\frac{1}{16}
,

(6)
|W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}v|\leq\bar{K}_{w}\|v\|_{1}
,
u^{\prime}(\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})(\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{\prime}u\leq K_{\alpha,2}\|u\|^{2}
.

With probability
1-\delta_{\alpha}
, for the with-covariate case, over all
i\in[N]
where
\|X_{i}-x\|\leq h
, we have:
(i)
\alpha_{*x}(X):=\alpha_{0}(x)+\nabla_{x}\alpha_{0}(x)(X-x)\in\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
,

(ii)
{\displaystyle\max_{j\in[p],\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})}}|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})|+|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i})|\leq M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}\|v\|
,

(iii)
|u^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i})|\leq\bar{K}_{w}\|u\|_{1}
,
|u^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}v|\leq\bar{K}_{w}\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{1}\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{1}
,

(iv)
\displaystyle\max_{j\in[p],\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})}|u^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W(\tilde{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i})v|+|u^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})v|\leq K_{\alpha,2}\|u\|\|v\|
,

(v)
{\displaystyle\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha}),\|\tilde{x}-x\|\leq h}}\|\tilde{\alpha}(\tilde{x})-\alpha_{0}(\tilde{x})\|\frac{M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}}{\xi}<1/4
,
{\displaystyle\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha}),\|\tilde{x}-x\|\leq h}}\|\tilde{\alpha}(\tilde{x})-\alpha_{0}(\tilde{x})\|^{2}\frac{K_{\alpha,2}}{\xi}\leq 1/16
,

(vi)
|W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}v|\leq\bar{K}_{w}\|v\|_{1}
,
u^{\prime}[\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i})][\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0}(X_{i}),Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(X_{i})]^{\prime}u\leq K_{\alpha,2}\|u\|^{2}
,
{\displaystyle\max_{j\in[p],k\in[M]}}|v^{\prime}\nabla_{x}^{2}r_{0j}(x)v|+|v^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\alpha_{0k}(x)v|\leq\bar{K}_{x,2}\|v\|^{2}
.

Without loss of generality, we focus on discussing conditions (ii)-(vi) for the with-covariate case. Given any
i\in[N],j\in[p],k\in[M]
,
\nabla_{\alpha_{k}}W_{j}(\alpha(X_{i}),Y_{i})=\mathbf{1}\{k\in\ell(j)\}W_{j}(\alpha(X_{i}),Y_{i})\frac{\partial_{\alpha_{k}}z_{k}(Y_{i},\alpha(X_{i}))}{z_{k}(Y_{i},\alpha(X_{i}))}
, where
\frac{\partial_{\alpha_{k}}z_{k}(Y_{i},\alpha(X_{i}))}{z_{k}(Y_{i},\alpha(X_{i}))}=\frac{1-2Y_{ik}}{2\alpha_{k}(X_{i})(1-\alpha_{k}(X_{i}))}
,
\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})=W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}(X_{i}),Y_{i})[g_{ij}g_{ij}^{\prime}+D_{ij}]
, where
g_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
and
g_{ijk}=\frac{\mathbf{1}\{k\in\ell(j)\}(1-2Y_{ik})}{2\tilde{\alpha}_{k}(X_{i})(1-\tilde{\alpha}_{k}(X_{i}))}
,
D_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times M}
is a diagonal matrix with
k
-th diagonal entry
-\frac{\mathbf{1}\{k\in\ell(j)\}(1-2Y_{ik})(1-2\tilde{\alpha}_{k}(X_{i}))}{2\tilde{\alpha}_{k}(X_{i})^{2}(1-\tilde{\alpha}_{k}(X_{i}))^{2}}
. Condition (vi) controls the curvature of
\alpha_{0}(\cdot)
and
r_{0}(\cdot)
with respect to the covariates.
Particularly, when
\alpha_{0k}(X_{i})
are bounded away from both
0
and
1
, since
\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
are close to
\alpha_{0}(\cdot)
with high probability by the construction of
\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
,
\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})
are also bounded away from both
0
and
1
with high probability, then condition (vi) implies that conditions (ii) – (iv) hold. The theoretical analysis later implies
{\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\tilde{x}-x\|\leq h\\
\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{x}(\hat{\alpha})\end{subarray}}}M^{1/2}\|\tilde{\alpha}(\tilde{x})-\alpha_{0}(\tilde{x})\|
goes to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
. So (v) holds when
N
is sufficiently large.

In the proofs of the theoretical results for the no-covariate case, whenever a result is stated under Assumption
3
, we directly invoke the corresponding technical conditions from Assumption
Appendix
(1) – (6). The former is stated in the main text to streamline the exposition, whereas the latter provides the detailed conditions used in the proofs.

Proof .1

Proof of Theorem
5.3

Firstly, define
W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,y):=W(\hat{\alpha},y)+\nabla_{\alpha}W(\hat{\alpha},y)(\alpha-\hat{\alpha})
,
Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,r):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log(1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,Y_{i})^{\prime}r)
,
\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\{\alpha_{0}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})\}
,
\mathcal{E}_{1}:=\{\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,r_{0})-Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})\geq-\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})\}
, where
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})=C\bar{K}_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(M/\delta)}{N}}\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}+\frac{CK_{\alpha,2}}{\xi^{2}}\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}
.
\mathcal{E}_{2}:=\{\frac{\lambda}{c}\geq\left\|{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)\nabla_{r}Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})\right\|_{\infty}\}
,
\mathcal{E}_{3}:=\{\min_{i\in[N]}1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\geq\xi/2\}
.
Assumption
Appendix
implies that
\mathcal{E}_{0}
holds with probability at least
1-\delta_{\alpha}
.
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds with probability
1-\epsilon-\delta_{\alpha}-\delta(1+\mathrm{o}(1))
by Lemma
EC.1.4
.
\mathcal{E}_{2}
holds with probability
1-\delta-\delta_{\alpha}
by the conditions of Theorem
5.3
and Lemma
EC.1.6
. Note that with probability
1-\epsilon-C\delta_{\alpha}
,

\min_{i\in[N]}1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\geq\min_{i\in[N]}1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}+(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\geq_{(1)}\frac{3}{4}\xi-M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}\|\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha}\|\geq_{(2)}\frac{1}{2}\xi
, where inequality (1) follows from Holder’s inequality and the fact that the proof of Proposition
5.2
shows that with probability
1-\epsilon-C^{\prime}\delta_{\alpha}
,
1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\geq\frac{3}{4}\xi
, and inequality (2) follows from Assumption
Appendix
. Let
\hat{r}=\hat{r}^{\mathrm{FO}}
. It is then sufficient to prove that the result holds on
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}
. Particularly on the intersection of all these events we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\displaystyle Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},\hat{r})-\lambda\|\hat{r}\|_{w,1}\displaystyle\geq_{(1)}\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,\hat{r})-\lambda\|\hat{r}\|_{w,1}\geq_{(2)}\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,r_{0})-\lambda\|r_{0}\|_{w,1}\\
&\displaystyle=\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\{Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,r_{0})-Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})\}+Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})-\lambda\|r_{0}\|_{w,1}\\
&\displaystyle\geq_{(3)}-\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})+Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})-\lambda\|r_{0}\|_{w,1},\end{array}

(17)

where (1) holds by
\mathcal{E}_{0}
, (2) holds by the optimality of
\hat{r}
and (3) holds by
\mathcal{E}_{1}
. Following similar proof steps as in Lemma
EC.1.12
,

Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},\hat{r})\leq Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})+\nabla_{r}Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})-\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}

for some universal constant
c_{0}>0
. Combining this and (
17
), on event
\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}
, using (weighted) Hölder’s inequality we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\displaystyle\quad\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}\leq\lambda\|r_{0}\|_{w,1}-\lambda\|\hat{r}\|_{w,1}+\nabla_{r}Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})\\
&\displaystyle\leq\lambda\|r_{0}\|_{w,1}-\lambda\|\hat{r}\|_{w,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})+\left\|{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)\nabla_{r}Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})\right\|_{\infty}\|\hat{r}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}\\
&\displaystyle\leq\lambda\|r_{0}\|_{w,1}-\lambda\|\hat{r}\|_{w,1}+\frac{\lambda}{c}\|\hat{r}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{c}\right)\lambda\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}\!-\!\!\lambda\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha}).\end{array}

(18)

Let
\mathcal{W}:={\rm diag}(w)
. Hölder’s inequality implies
v^{\prime}\hat{U}v\geq v^{\prime}Uv-\|v\|_{w,1}^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(U-\hat{U})\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}
.
Define
\displaystyle U:=\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}
,
\displaystyle\hat{U}:=\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}}{(1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}
, we have

\frac{c_{0}}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\!\left\{\!\frac{W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\!\right\}^{2}\!\!\!\!-\!\frac{c_{0}}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\!\left\{\!\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\!\right\}^{2}\!\!\!\geq\displaystyle-\|\hat{r}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(U-\hat{U})\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}

(19)

Recall that
T
is the support of
r_{0}
. For the rest of the proof, we show that the result holds under two exclusive cases:
\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}\geq 2\frac{c+1}{c-1}\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}
and
\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}<2\frac{c+1}{c-1}\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}
. First assume that
\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}\geq 2\frac{c+1}{c-1}\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}
.
From (
18
) we have

\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})-\frac{\lambda}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}.

(20)

Thus from (
19
) and (
20
) we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\displaystyle\quad\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}-\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}\\
&\displaystyle\geq-\|\hat{r}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(U-\hat{U})\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}=-(\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}+\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1})^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(U-\hat{U})\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}\\
&\displaystyle\geq-\left(\frac{1}{2\frac{c+1}{c-1}}+1\right)^{2}\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(U-\hat{U})\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}\geq_{(a)}-\frac{9}{4}\ \frac{4\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{2}(\hat{\alpha})}{\mbox{$(1-\frac{1}{c})^{2}$}\lambda^{2}}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(U-\hat{U})\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty},\end{array}

where (a) follows since (
20
) implies that
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})\geq\frac{\lambda}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}
when
\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}\geq 2\frac{c+1}{c-1}\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}
. Rearranging the terms above and using (
20
) again we have
\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}\leq\frac{9\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{2}(\hat{\alpha})\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}}{\mbox{$(1-\frac{1}{c})^{2}$}\lambda^{2}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})
. Next suppose that
\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}<2\frac{c+1}{c-1}\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}
. Then

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{N}\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\left\{\!\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\!\right\}^{2}\!\!\!\!\leq_{(1)}\!\!\frac{\lambda(1+c)\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}}{c}\!+\!\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})\!+\!\|\hat{r}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}\\
&\displaystyle\leq_{(2)}\frac{\lambda(1+c)\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{c_{0}}c\kappa_{\bar{c}}}\!\!\sqrt{\frac{c_{0}}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}}\\
&\displaystyle\quad+\frac{s\left(2\frac{c+1}{c-1}+1\right)^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}}{c_{0}\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{2}}\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha}),\end{array}

where (1) follows by (
18
) and (
19
), and (2) follows from the definition of the restricted eigenvalue,
\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}\leq\sqrt{s}\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,2}
and
\|\hat{r}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}^{2}=(\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}\|_{w,1}+\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1})^{2}\leq\left(2\frac{c+1}{c-1}+1\right)^{2}\|\hat{r}_{T}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}^{2}
. Note that
\frac{s\left(2\frac{c+1}{c-1}+1\right)^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}}{c_{0}\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{2}}\leq_{(i)}\frac{Cs\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}K_{\alpha,2}}{\xi\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{2}\min_{j\in[p]}w_{j}^{2}}\leq_{(ii)}C\delta_{N}
, where
C\geq\frac{\left(2\frac{c+1}{c-1}+1\right)^{2}}{c_{0}}\max_{k,l\in[p]}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}5\left(1+\frac{|W_{k}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})|}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right)\left(1+\frac{|W_{l}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})|}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right)
.
Here the inequality (i) holds by Lemma
EC.1.8
, and the inequality (ii) holds by the condition in Theorem
5.3
. Since
\delta_{N}\rightarrow 0
, so for
N
sufficiently large,
\frac{s\left(2\frac{c+1}{c-1}+1\right)^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}}{c_{0}\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
Furthermore, note that

\frac{x^{2}}{2}\leq\lambda(1+\frac{1}{c})\frac{\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{c_{0}}\kappa_{\bar{c}}}x+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})\Rightarrow x\leq 2\lambda(1+\frac{1}{c})\frac{\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{c_{0}}\kappa_{\bar{c}}}+\sqrt{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{1/2}(\hat{\alpha})
for
x=\sqrt{\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}}
.
So the result holds.

\blacksquare

Proof .2

Proof of Corollary
5.4

According to (
13
),
\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})=\prod_{j=1}^{M}[\hat{\alpha}_{j}-cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}\hat{s}_{j},\hat{\alpha}_{j}+cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}\hat{s}_{j}]
, where
\hat{s}_{j}=\sqrt{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}
and
cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}:=(1-\delta_{\alpha})\mbox{-quantile of}\max_{j\in[M]}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{i}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}{\sqrt{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}}\right|
conditioning on
\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
.
Denote
\hat{s}=(\hat{s}_{j})_{j\in[M]}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
. Then for any
t>0
,

\begin{array}[]{rl}\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\displaystyle\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\left\lVert\alpha-\alpha_{0}\right\rVert_{2}\geq t\bigg)&\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\left\lVert\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\right\rVert_{2}+cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}\sqrt{\sum_{j\in[M]}\hat{s}_{j}^{2}}\geq t\bigg)\\
&\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\left\lVert\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\right\rVert_{2}\geq t/2)+\mathbb{P}(cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}\sqrt{M/4}\geq t/2),\end{array}

(21)

where the second inequality above follows by triangular inequality and the fact that
\hat{s}_{j}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}
for all
j\in[M]
. To bound
\mathbb{P}(\left\lVert\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\right\rVert_{2}\geq t/2)
of (
21
), note that by Proposition
EC.3.1
, with probability
\geq 1-\delta
,
\left\lVert\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\right\rVert_{2}\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\frac{M\log(1/\delta)}{N}}
, where
c_{1}
is some absolute constant. To bound
\mathbb{P}(cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}\sqrt{M/4}\geq t/2)
of (
21
), note that
\xi_{i}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, therefore conditional on
\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{i}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}{\sqrt{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})^{2}}{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}\right)
holds for all
j\in[M]
, so
\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{j\in[M]}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{i}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}{\sqrt{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}}\right|\right]\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\sigma^{2}\log(2M)}{N}}
, where
\sigma^{2}=\max_{j\in[M]}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})^{2}}{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}
.
By Lemma
EC.1.3
, for some absolute constant
C_{0}
, with probability
1-\delta
we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}\displaystyle\max_{j\in[M]}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{i}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}{\sqrt{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}}\right|&\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{j\in[M]}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\xi_{i}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}{\sqrt{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}}\right|\right]\!\!+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2\log\left(2/\delta\right)}{N}}\\
&\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log(M/\delta)}{N}}\sqrt{\max_{j\in[M]}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{(Y_{ij}-\hat{\alpha}_{j})^{2}}{\hat{\alpha}_{j}(1-\hat{\alpha}_{j})}}.\end{array}

Let
\delta\leq\delta_{\alpha}
and set
t=C\sqrt{\frac{M\log(M/\delta)}{N}}
for some absolute constant
C
in (
21
), then with probability
1-2\delta
,
\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\left\lVert\alpha-\alpha_{0}\right\rVert_{2}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{M\log(M/\delta)}{N}}
. Additionally, for any
t>0
, by triangular inequality,

\begin{array}[]{rl}\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}\geq t\big)&\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}\geq t/2)+\mathbb{P}(cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}\|\hat{s}\|_{1}\geq t/2)\\
&\displaystyle\leq_{(a)}\mathbb{P}(\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}\geq t/2)+\mathbb{P}(cv_{\delta_{\alpha}}(M/2)\geq t/2),\end{array}

(22)

where (a) follows because
\|\hat{s}\|_{1}\leq M/2
.
By Proposition
EC.3.1
, with probability
1-\delta
, we have
\left\lVert\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\right\rVert_{1}\leq c_{1}\frac{M\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{N}}
for some constant
c_{1}>0
. Let
\delta\leq\delta_{\alpha}
and set
t=CM\sqrt{\frac{\log(M/\delta)}{N}}
for some absolute constant
C
, following (
22
), with probability
1-2\delta
, we have
\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}\leq CM\sqrt{\frac{\log(M/\delta)}{N}}
. Additionally, we choose
\lambda
satisfying the condition of Theorem
5.3
, so that for some absolute constant
\bar{C}
,
\lambda\leq\bar{C}N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(p/\delta)}
, then under the condition
\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{-1}+\bar{K}_{2}+K_{\alpha,1}+K_{\alpha,2}/\xi^{2}\leq C^{\prime}
, we have
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\hat{\alpha})\leq\bar{C}^{\prime}M\sqrt{\frac{\log(M/\delta)}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(M/\delta)}{N}}+\bar{C}^{\prime}\frac{M\log(M/\delta)}{N}
for some constant
\bar{C}^{\prime}
. By Theorem
5.3
, for an absolute constant
C
,

\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{FO}}-r_{0})\}^{2}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{s\log(p/\delta)}{N}}+C\sqrt{\frac{M\log(M/\delta)}{N}}

holds with probability
1-C\delta-C\delta_{\alpha}-C\epsilon
, hence the result follows since
p=2^{M}-M-1
.
\blacksquare

Electronic Companion to “Adversarial Estimation of Binary Configuration Probabilities under Sparse Dependence”

Appendix EC.1

Additional Proofs and Results for Section
5.1

Proof EC.1.1

Proof of Proposition
5.2

By Lemma
EC.1.6
, with probability
1-\epsilon-\delta_{\alpha}-\delta(1+\mathrm{o}(1))
, we have

\left\|\frac{{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\!\frac{W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})}{1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\leq\left\|\frac{{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\!\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\!\!\!\!+C_{1}(1+\beta_{N})\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|\frac{M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}}{\xi}
. So by the lower-bound condition on
\lambda
,
\left\|\frac{{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})}{1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\lambda}{c}
. Let
\mathcal{W}:={\rm diag}(w)
. By Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle v^{\prime}\hat{U}v\geq v^{\prime}Uv-\|v\|_{w,1}^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(U-\hat{U})\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}.

(23)

We apply (
23
) for

U:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}},\ \hat{U}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}}{(1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}
. Note that with probability
1-\delta_{\alpha}
,
1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\geq_{(1)}1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}-|(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}|\geq_{(2)}\xi-M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|\geq_{(3)}\frac{3}{4}\xi>0
, where (1) follows from Taylor expansion and
\tilde{\alpha}
is on the line segment between
\alpha_{0},\hat{\alpha}
. So when
\alpha_{0}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
, by the construction of
\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
,
\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
. Hence following from Assumption
3
and Assumption
Appendix
,
\min_{i\in[N]}1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\geq\frac{3}{4}\xi>0
with probability
1-\epsilon-3\delta_{\alpha}
. Hence with probability
1-C\epsilon-C\delta_{\alpha}-C\delta(1+\mathrm{o}(1))
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\!\left\{\!\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\!\right\}^{2}\!\!\!\!\!\leq_{(1)}\!\!\frac{c_{0}}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\!\left\{\!\frac{W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0})}{1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\!\right\}^{2}\!\!\!\!\!+\!c_{0}\|\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}\!\!\!-\!r_{0}\|_{w,1}^{2}\!\|\!\mathcal{W}^{-1}\!(\hat{U}\!-\!U)\!\mathcal{W}^{-1}\!\|_{\infty,\infty}\\
&\displaystyle\leq_{(2)}2\lambda\frac{c+1}{c-1}\left\lVert\hat{r}_{T}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\right\rVert_{w,1}+c_{0}\left(\frac{2c}{c-1}\right)^{2}\|\hat{r}_{T}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}^{2}\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}\!(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}\\
&\displaystyle\leq_{(3)}\frac{2\lambda\sqrt{s}(c+1)/(c-1)}{\kappa_{\bar{c}}\sqrt{c_{0}}}\sqrt{\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}}\\
&\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{4c^{2}s\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}}{(c-1)^{2}\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{2}}\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2},\end{array}

where (1) follows by (
23
). The first term of (2) follows since by Lemma
EC.1.12
, for some universal constant
c_{0}>0
, with probability
1-\epsilon-\delta_{\alpha}-\delta(1+\mathrm{o}(1))
, we have
\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\left\{\frac{W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0})}{(1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})}\right\}^{2}\!\!\leq 2\lambda\frac{c+1}{c-1}\left\lVert\hat{r}_{T}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\right\rVert_{w,1}
. The second term of (2) follows since
\|\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}=\|\hat{r}_{T}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}+\|\hat{r}_{T^{c}}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}\leq\frac{2c}{c-1}\|\hat{r}_{T}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}
according to Lemma
EC.1.10
. (3) follows from the definition of restricted eigenvalues and
\|\hat{r}_{T}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\|_{w,1}\leq\sqrt{s}\|\hat{r}_{T}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0}\|_{w,2}
. By Taylor expansion, for each
j\in[p]
,
i\in[N]
there exists
\tilde{\alpha}^{ji}
on the line segment between
\hat{\alpha}
and
\alpha_{0}
such that

W_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})=W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})+(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}^{ji},Y_{i})
. Simiarly, for some
\tilde{\alpha}
on the line segment between
\hat{\alpha}
and
\alpha_{0}
, for any
j\in[p]
,

\begin{array}[]{rl}\displaystyle\frac{W_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})}{1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}&\displaystyle=\frac{W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})+(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}^{ji},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}+(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\\
&\displaystyle=\frac{W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\left(1-\frac{(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}+(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right)\\
&\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}^{ji},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}+(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}=\frac{W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}+\widetilde{R}_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i}).\end{array}

By the construction of
\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
as shown later in Section
5.3
,
\hat{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
. Particularly,
|(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}|\leq_{(a)}K_{\alpha,1}\sqrt{M}\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|\leq_{(b)}K_{\alpha,1}c_{1}\bigg(\frac{M}{\sqrt{N}}+\sqrt{\frac{M\log(1/\delta)}{N}}\bigg)<_{(c)}\frac{1}{2}\xi
, where (a) holds with probability
1-\delta_{\alpha}
from Assumption
Appendix
, (b) holds with probability
1-\delta
according to Proposition
EC.3.1
, (c) holds when
N
is sufficiently large. Since
1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}>\xi
with probability
1-\epsilon
,
|\widetilde{R}_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})|\leq\frac{CK_{\alpha,1}}{\xi}\left(1+\frac{|W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})|}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right)M^{1/2}\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|
with probability
1-\delta_{\alpha}-\delta-\epsilon
when
N
is sufficiently large, where
C
is an absolute constant.
Define
W_{i}:=\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}
,
\hat{W}_{i}:=\frac{W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})}{1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}
. For any
l\in[p]
, define
\tilde{R}_{il}:=\tilde{R}_{l}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})
. By Assumption
Appendix
, with probability
1-\delta_{\alpha}
,
\left(1+\frac{|W_{l}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})|}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right)\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|\frac{M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}}{\xi}\leq\frac{1}{4}\left(1+\frac{|W_{l}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})|}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right)
, and
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\tilde{R}_{il}\tilde{R}_{ik}|\leq\frac{C}{4N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(1+|W_{il}|)|\tilde{R}_{ik}|
. Note
|\hat{W}_{il}\hat{W}_{ik}-W_{il}W_{ik}|\leq\left|W_{il}\tilde{R}_{ik}+\tilde{R}_{il}W_{ik}+\tilde{R}_{il}\tilde{R}_{ik}\right|
. Thus with probability
1-C^{\prime}\delta_{\alpha}-C^{\prime}\delta-C^{\prime}\epsilon
,
\displaystyle\left|U_{lk}-\hat{U}_{lk}\right|\leq C^{\prime}\frac{M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}}{\xi}\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|\max_{l,k\in[p]}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(1+|W_{il}|\right)\left(1+|W_{ik}|\right)
for some constant
C^{\prime}
independent of
s,N,M
, so the given conditions further imply that for some constant
\bar{C}
independent of
s,N,M
, we have
s\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}/\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{2}\leq\bar{C}sM^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|/\{\xi\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{2}\min_{j\in[p]}w_{j}^{2}\}\leq\delta_{N}\bar{C}
.
Recall that
\delta_{N}\rightarrow 0
. So for sufficiently large
N
,
4c^{2}s\|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\hat{U}-U)\mathcal{W}^{-1}\|_{\infty,\infty}/\{\kappa_{\bar{c}}^{2}(c-1)^{2}\}\leq 1/2
. Let
x=\sqrt{\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}}
. Note that
\frac{x^{2}}{2}\leq\frac{2\lambda\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{c_{0}}\kappa_{\bar{c}}}\frac{c+1}{c-1}x\Rightarrow x\leq\frac{4\lambda\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{c_{0}}\kappa_{\bar{c}}}\frac{c+1}{c-1}
. This implies

\sqrt{\frac{c_{0}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\{\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\}^{2}}\leq\frac{4\lambda\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{c_{0}}\kappa_{\bar{c}}}\frac{c+1}{c-1}
. By Proposition
EC.3.1
, with probability
1-\delta
,
\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|\leq c_{1}\big(\sqrt{\frac{M}{N}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{N}}\big)
for some
c_{1}>0
. So the result follows.
\blacksquare

Example EC.1.2

Let
M^{6}=\mathrm{o}(N)
and
M\to\infty
,
\alpha_{0j}=\frac{1}{2},j\in[M]
. Here we use
r_{0\ell}
to denote the coordinate of
r_{0}
corresponding to configuration
\ell
. For a fixed constant
a>0
, let
r_{0\ell}=a\,M/\sqrt{N}
when
|\ell|=2
, and
r_{0\ell}=0
when
|\ell|\geq 3
. So
s=\binom{M}{2}
. So
\mathbb{P}(Y=y)=2^{-M}[1+\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq M}r_{0,jk}(2y_{j}-1)(2y_{k}-1)],\forall y\in\{0,1\}^{M}
. Particularly, the probability is well defined since
1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}=(1+a\frac{M}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{j<k}(2Y_{ij}-1)(2Y_{ik}-1))=1+\mathrm{o}(1)
. Also
\sup_{y}|W(\alpha_{0},y)^{\prime}r_{0}|\leq\frac{aM}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{M^{2}}{2}=\frac{aM^{3}}{2\sqrt{N}}=\mathrm{o}(1)
. Proposition
EC.3.1
implies that
\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|\asymp\sqrt{M/N}
. Proposition
5.2
requires
\lambda=\bar{C}M/\sqrt{N}
for some absolute constant
\bar{C}
. By smoothness conditions,
\bigl\|\mathbb{E}[W(\hat{\alpha},Y)]-\mathbb{E}[W(\alpha_{0},Y)]\bigr\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{M}{N}}
. Additionally,
\|\mathbb{E}[W(\alpha_{0},Y)]\|_{\infty}=\max_{\ell}|r_{0\ell}|=a\,\frac{M}{\sqrt{N}}
. Hence
\|\mathbb{E}[W(\hat{\alpha},Y)]\|_{\infty}\leq a\,\frac{M}{\sqrt{N}}+C\sqrt{\frac{M}{N}}\leq\frac{\lambda}{4}
for
\bar{C}
sufficiently large. Let
\hat{Q}(r)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log\bigl(1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r\bigr)-\lambda\|r\|_{1}
. Since the coordinates of
W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})
are uniformly bounded,
Hoeffding’s inequality and a union bound over
p\leq 2^{M}
coordinates yield
\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})-\mathbb{E}[W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})]\right\|_{\infty}=\mathrm{O}_{\mathrm{P}}\!(\sqrt{M/N})=\mathrm{o}_{\mathrm{P}}\!(M/\sqrt{N})
. Then the smoothness conditions imposed imply
\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})-\mathbb{E}[W(\hat{\alpha},Y)]\right\|_{\infty}=\mathrm{O}_{\mathrm{P}}\!(\sqrt{M/N})=\mathrm{o}_{\mathrm{P}}\!(M/\sqrt{N})
. Therefore, with probability tending to one,
\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}
. On this event, for any feasible
r\neq 0
,
\hat{Q}(r)-\hat{Q}(0)\leq\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})\right)^{\prime}r-\lambda\|r\|_{1}\leq\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})\right\|_{\infty}\|r\|_{1}-\lambda\|r\|_{1}\leq-\frac{\lambda}{2}\|r\|_{1}<0
. Hence
\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}=0
with probability tending to one. Further, on this event,
\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}(\hat{r}^{\mathrm{PI}}-r_{0})\}^{2}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\}^{2}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}}
. Note that
r_{0}
’s support corresponds to all the bundles
|\ell|=2
. Let
\Sigma=\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{W}_{i}\widetilde{W}_{i}^{\prime}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times s}
, where
\widetilde{W}_{i}
denotes the subvector of
W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})
restricted to coordinates corresponding to configurations with
|\ell|=2
only, and
s={M\choose 2}
. Index the
s
configurations with
|\ell|=2
by edges
e=(j,k)
of the complete graph
K_{M}
, where
j,k\in[M]
and
j\neq k
. Then
\Sigma=\mathbf{I}_{s}+\frac{aM}{\sqrt{N}}A
. Here
\mathbf{I}_{s}
is the
s\times s
identity matrix, and
A
is the adjacency matrix for the line graph of
K_{M}
, where
A_{e,e^{\prime}}=1
iff the two edges
e,e^{\prime}
share a vertex. For
M
large,
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)
(i.e. the minimum eigenvalue of
\Sigma
) satisfies
\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)=1-\frac{2aM}{\sqrt{N}}\geq 1/2
since
M^{6}=\mathrm{o}(N)
. By applying a standard matrix Chernoff lower-tail inequality, with high probability we get
\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{W}_{i}\tilde{W}_{i}^{\prime}\right)\geq c_{0}
for some absolute constant
c_{0}>0
. Note
1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}=1+\frac{aM}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{j<k}(2Y_{ij}-1)(2Y_{ik}-1)\in[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
for
M
sufficiently large. Thus
\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\}^{2}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}}\geq\frac{2}{3}\sqrt{r_{0T}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{W}_{i}\widetilde{W}_{i}^{\prime}\right)r_{0T}}\geq\Omega_{p}\left(M\sqrt{s/N}\right)
.

EC.1.1

Technical Lemmas and Additional Results for Section
5.1

Lemma EC.1.3

(Borell-TIS inequality
(
Borell 1975
)
)

Let
X
be a centered Gaussian random variable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\displaystyle\sigma^{2}:=\max_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]
. Then
\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|\max_{i\in[n]}X_{i}-\mathbb{E}\big[\max_{i\in[n]}X_{i}\big]\bigg|>t\bigg)\leq 2e^{-t^{2}/2\sigma^{2}}
holds for every
t>0
.

Lemma EC.1.4

Suppose Assumptions
3
,
Appendix
hold. Then
\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,r_{0})-Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})\geq-C\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}-C\frac{K_{\alpha,2}}{\xi^{2}}\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}
holds with probability
1-\epsilon-\delta_{\alpha}
, where
C
is an absolute constant independent of
s,N,M
. Moreover, under Assumption
3
,
\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2M)C^{\prime}\bar{K}_{2}\sqrt{1/N}
holds with probability
1-\delta((1+\mathrm{o}(1)))
for some universal constant
C^{\prime}
.

Proof EC.1.5

Proof of Lemma
EC.1.4

Recall that
Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,r)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log(1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,Y_{i})^{\prime}r)
where
W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,y)=W(\hat{\alpha},y)+\nabla_{\alpha}W(\hat{\alpha},y)(\alpha-\hat{\alpha})
. Suppose all conditions of Assumption
Appendix
hold and condition (iii) of Assumption
3
holds. Then for any
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
,

Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,r_{0})-Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log\left(1+(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\frac{\nabla_{\alpha}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right).

(24)

By the mean value theorem,
(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}=(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}+(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\{\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W(\bar{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\}(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})
for some
\bar{\alpha}
,
\tilde{\alpha}
in the line segment between
\hat{\alpha}
and
\alpha_{0}
. Further,
W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}=W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}+(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}=_{(1)}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})^{\prime}\{\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\}(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})
, and
\frac{1}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}=\frac{1}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}+\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}-W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})(1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})}=\frac{\left(1-\Delta R_{0}\right)}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}
, where (1) follows from the Taylor expansion of
W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}
around
\hat{\alpha}
, and
\Delta R_{0}:=-1/2[(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})]/\{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\}
. Thus we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}\displaystyle\frac{(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}&\displaystyle\!\!=\!\!\frac{[\!(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\!+\!(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\!\{\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W(\bar{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\}(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})\!]\!(1-\Delta R_{0})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\\
&\displaystyle\!\!=\!\!\frac{(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}+\widetilde{R}(\alpha,\hat{\alpha},Y_{i}),\end{array}

(25)

where
|\widetilde{R}(\alpha,\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})|\leq_{(i)}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|\frac{M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}}{\xi}|\Delta R_{0}|+\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}2\|\tilde{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}\frac{K_{\alpha,2}}{\xi}(1+|\Delta R_{0}|)\leq_{(ii)}3\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\tilde{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}\frac{K_{\alpha,2}}{\xi^{2}}<_{(iii)}\frac{3}{16}
. Here inequality (i) uses condition (iii) of Assumption
3
and (2), (4) of Assumption
Appendix
. Since
\hat{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})
, then (2), (4), (5) of Assumption
Appendix
imply
|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}|\leq K_{\alpha,2}\|v\|
and
|W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}-W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}|\leq\frac{1}{16}\xi
, which then imply that
|\Delta R_{0}|\leq\frac{16}{15}\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\tilde{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}\frac{K_{\alpha,2}}{\xi}
, and (5) of Assumption
Appendix
further implies inequality (ii). inequality (iii) follows from (5) of Assumption
Appendix
. Next, note that
\log(1+x)\geq\frac{x}{1+x}=x-\frac{x^{2}}{1+x}
and conditions (2), (4), (5) of Assumption
Appendix
imply that
\left|\frac{(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}+\widetilde{R}(\alpha,\hat{\alpha},Y_{i}))\right|\leq 1/2
. Further by (
24
) and (
25
), for a universal constant
C
,

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\quad Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,r_{0})-Q^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},r_{0})\\
&\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}+\widetilde{R}(\alpha,\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})-\!\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}+\widetilde{R}(\alpha,\hat{\alpha},Y_{i}))\right)^{2}\\
&\displaystyle\geq-C\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|_{1}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}-C\frac{K_{\alpha,2}}{\xi^{2}}\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|^{2},\end{array}

where the second inequality follows from Hölder’s inequality, the bound derived above for
|\widetilde{R}(\alpha,\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})|
and Assumption
Appendix
. The first statement then holds since (iii) of Assumption
3
holds with probability
1-\epsilon
and the conditions of Assumption
Appendix
hold with probability
1-\delta_{\alpha}
. The second statement of the Lemma follows from an application of self-normalized moderate deviation theory. Let
\tilde{X}_{ij}:=\nabla_{\alpha_{j}}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}/\{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}\}
. so under Assumptions
3
,
3
we have
\min_{j\in[M]}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{X}_{ij}^{2}]\geq\bar{\psi}_{2}^{2}
,
\max_{j\in[M]}\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{X}_{ij}|^{3}]^{1/3}\leq\bar{K}_{3}
and
\sqrt{\frac{\log^{3}p}{N}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{M^{3}}{N}}\leq\delta_{N}
, thus

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{X}_{ij}\right|>\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2M)}{N^{1/2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{X}_{ij}^{2}}\mbox{ for some }j\in[M]\right)\\
&\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[M]}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{X}_{ij}\right|>\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/2M)}{N^{1/2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{X}_{ij}^{2}}\right)\leq\delta(1+\mathrm{o}(1)),\end{array}

where the first inequality follows from the union bound, and the second inequality follows by applying Theorem 2.3 in
Jing et al. (2003)
. Hence the result follows.

\blacksquare

Lemma EC.1.6

Define
\displaystyle\beta_{N}:=\max_{j\in[p]}\frac{1}{w_{j}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{|W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})|}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}
.
Suppose Assumption
3
and (i)–(iv):

(i)
\displaystyle\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha}),j\in[p]}|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\alpha,Y_{i})|\leq M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}\|v\|
, (ii)
\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha}),j\in[p]}|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})u|\leq K_{\alpha,2}\|v\|\ \|u\|
, (iii)
\displaystyle|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}|\leq M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}\|v\|
,
\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}|v^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}v|\leq K_{\alpha,2}\|v\|^{2}
, (iv)
\displaystyle\|\alpha-\alpha_{0}\|M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}/\xi<1/4
,
4\max_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}(\hat{\alpha})}\|\tilde{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}K_{\alpha,2}/\xi\leq 1/4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})}{1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\!\leq\left\|\frac{{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)}{N}\!\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\!+\!\frac{C(1+\beta_{N})M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|}{\xi}\\
&\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\!\frac{W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\!\leq\left\|\frac{{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\!+\!\frac{C(1+\beta_{N})K_{\alpha,2}\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|^{2}}{\xi}\end{array}

where
C
is an absolute constant in the above,
w=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{p})
, and for each
j\in[p]
,
w_{j}
is the weight for (
3
) and (
6
). Moreover, with probability
1-\delta(1+\mathrm{o}(1))
,
\left\|\frac{{\rm diag}^{-1}(w)}{N}\!\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\!\!\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right\|_{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\!\leq\frac{\Phi^{-1}(1-\delta/(2p))}{\sqrt{N}}\max_{j\in[p]}\frac{1}{w_{j}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{W_{j}^{2}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})^{2}}}
holds under Assumptions
3
and
3
.

Proof EC.1.7

Proof of Lemma
EC.1.6

Note that by Taylor expansion, for each
j\in[p],i\in[N]
there exist
\tilde{\alpha}^{ji}
,
\tilde{\alpha}
in the line segment between
\hat{\alpha}
and
\alpha_{0}
such that

W_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})=W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})+(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}^{ji},Y_{i})
. Thus

\begin{array}[]{rl}\displaystyle\frac{W_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})}{1+W(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}&\displaystyle=\frac{W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\left(1-\frac{(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}+(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right)\\
&\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\tilde{\alpha}^{ji},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}+(\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W(\tilde{\alpha},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}=\frac{W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}+\widetilde{R}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i}),\end{array}

where
\displaystyle|\widetilde{R}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})|\leq 2\left(1+\frac{|W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})|}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}\right)\frac{\|\hat{\alpha}-\alpha_{0}\|M^{1/2}K_{\alpha,1}}{\xi}
following from (iii) and (iv). So the first inequality holds.

Next we prove the second inequality. By Taylor expansion, for each
j\in[p],i\in[n]
there is
\bar{\alpha}_{ji}
in the line segment between
\hat{\alpha}
and
\alpha_{0}
such that

W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})=W_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})+(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})+\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W_{j}(\bar{\alpha}_{ji},Y_{i})(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})
. Recall that
W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha,y)=W(\hat{\alpha},y)+\nabla_{\alpha}W(\hat{\alpha},y)(\alpha-\hat{\alpha})
. Thus

\begin{array}[]{rl}W^{\mathrm{FO}}_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})&=W_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})+(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}W_{j}(\hat{\alpha},Y_{i})\\
&=W_{j}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})^{\prime}\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}W_{j}(\bar{\alpha}_{ji},Y_{i})(\alpha_{0}-\hat{\alpha})\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}&\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}+\frac{W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}-W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0}}{(1+W(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})(1+W^{\mathrm{FO}}(\alpha_{0},Y_{i})^{\prime}r_{0})}.\ \mbox{So}\end{array}
